COLPRONE (medrogestone)

No new data have been identified that are liable to modify the Committee’s assessment of the clinical
benefit  of  the  proprietary  medicinal  product  COLPRONE  (medrogestone)  in  the  indication  “artificial
cycle in combination with an oestrogen” in its opinion of 15 January 2025:
  Endometrial hyperplasia increases the risk of endometrial cancer.
  This is a preventive medicinal product.
  The efficacy/adverse effects ratio has not been adequately established, with the menopause

being considered to be the period at highest risk of developing meningioma.

  HAS • MEDICINAL PRODUCTS INDICATED IN HORMONAL REPLACEMENT THERAPY FOR THE MENOPAUSE*, oral or vaginal soft
capsules, tablets, coated tablets, film-coated tablets, scored tablets, transdermal devices, gel for cutaneous application in a bottle with dos-
5
ing pump • September 2025

  Considering  the  identified  meningioma  risk,  there  is  no  role  in  the  care  pathway  for
medrogestone in combination with an oestrogen in the context of hormone replacement therapy
in postmenopausal women.

  Public health benefit

Considering:

‒
‒
‒

the seriousness of the disease and its prevalence/incidence,
the partially met medical need,
the lack of response to the identified need, given the identification of a risk of the development
of meningioma requiring the implementation of monitoring measures, with the performance of
brain imaging by MRI, a potential negative impact of COLPRONE (medrogestone) is expected
on morbidity and mortality, quality of life and the organisation of care,

COLPRONE (medrogestone) is unlikely to have an additional impact on public health.

Considering all these elements, the Committee deems that the clinical benefit of COLPRONE
5  mg  (medrogestone)  remains insufficient to justify  public funding in  the MA  indication in
view of the available alternatives.

The  Committee  issues  an  unfavourable  opinion  for  inclusion  of  COLPRONE  5  mg
(medrogestone) in both the list of covered drugs for outpatients and the list of covered drugs
for  inpatients  approved  for  use  in  the  indication  “artificial  cycle  in  combination  with  an
oestrogen” and at the MA dosages.

Clinical Added Value

Considering:

‒  experience with the use of hormone replacement therapies for menopause and all the available
data, which do not call into question the efficacy of HRT in alleviating the climacteric symptoms
of menopause in the short term or its benefits for bone health in the longer term,
this efficacy, to be weighed up against the multiple risks of these treatments across different
target  areas:  cardiovascular,  venous  thromboembolic,  and  carcinogenic  risks  relating  to  the
breast (with the risk of breast cancer having been confirmed since the Committee’s previous
reassessment in 2014), ovaries and endometrium,

‒

‒

‒  publications suggesting that there may be a risk of a difference in effect between the oral and
transdermal routes, in particular the risk of thromboembolic disease or stroke, which appears to
be lower by the cutaneous route compared to the oral route; however, the summaries of product
characteristics of the proprietary medicinal products concerned do not reflect these differences,
the  risk  of  meningioma,  newly
identified  with  certain  progestogens  (chlormadinone,
nomegestrol, medrogestone), which does not, on the basis of current knowledge, constitute a
signal for the progestogens used in HRT, namely cyproterone acetate (at the 1 mg strength,
this  substance  having  been  identified  as  being  associated  with  a  risk  of  meningioma  at  the
25 mg  strength),  medroxyprogesterone  acetate  (at  the  2.5  or  5  mg  strength,  this  substance
having been identified as being associated with a risk of meningioma at the 150 mg strength),
dydrogesterone,  progesterone,  levonorgestrel;  the  SmPCs for  proprietary  medicinal  products
containing dydrogesterone and cyproterone acetate include warnings and contraindications with
